$ Bash
Add --cwd override and username guard to the scrubber; re-scrub
cd ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer-wp06 && python3 - <<'PYEOF'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('scripts/scrub-capture.py'); s = p.read_text()
def rep(old, new):
    global s; assert s.count(old) == 1, old[:50]; s = s.replace(old, new)
rep('''- `cwd` is rewritten to `~/…` and `ai-title` gets the title you pass.''',
    '''- `cwd` is rewritten to `~/…` (or replaced outright with `--cwd`) and
  `ai-title` gets the title you pass.
- Finally the output is grepped for the local username; a hit fails the run.''')
rep('''            if k == "cwd" and isinstance(v, str):
                out[k] = HOME_RE.sub("~", v)
                continue''', '''            if k == "cwd" and isinstance(v, str):
                out[k] = ctx["cwd"] if ctx["cwd"] is not None else HOME_RE.sub("~", v)
                continue''')
rep('''    ap.add_argument("--name", default="session.jsonl", help="output file name for the main log")''',
    '''    ap.add_argument("--name", default="session.jsonl", help="output file name for the main log")
    ap.add_argument("--cwd", default=None, help="replace every cwd with this value (e.g. ~/projects/tinycache)")''')
rep('''    ctx = {"title": args.title, "scrubbed": 0, "out_dir": out_dir}''',
    '''    ctx = {"title": args.title, "scrubbed": 0, "out_dir": out_dir, "cwd": args.cwd}''')
rep('''    (out_dir / "capture.json").write_text(json.dumps(capture, indent=2) + "\\n", encoding="utf-8")
    print(f"scrubbed {len(files)} file(s), {ctx['scrubbed']} values → {out_dir}")
    return 0''', '''    (out_dir / "capture.json").write_text(json.dumps(capture, indent=2) + "\\n", encoding="utf-8")

    # Belt and braces: the local username must not survive anywhere (paths
    # inside ids, slugs, or fields the allowlist did not anticipate).
    username = os.environ.get("USER") or os.environ.get("USERNAME") or ""
    if len(username) >= 3:
        for path in out_dir.rglob("*"):
            if path.is_file() and username in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"):
                print(f"error: username survives in {path}; pass --cwd or extend the scrub rules", file=sys.stderr)
                return 1
    print(f"scrubbed {len(files)} file(s), {ctx['scrubbed']} values → {out_dir}")
    return 0''')
p.write_text(s)
print('patched')
PYEOF

$ Bash
Print per-request usage for both gap-heavy captures
cd ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer-wp06 && for n in gap-heavy-5m gap-heavy-1h; do echo "== $n"; python3 - "$n" <<'EOF'
import json, sys, datetime
seen=set(); prev=None
for line in open(f'fixtures/captured/scenarios/{sys.argv[1]}/session.jsonl'):
    d=json.loads(line)
    if d.get('type')!='assistant': continue
    m=d['message']; mid=m['id']
    if mid in seen or m.get('model')=='<synthetic>': continue
    seen.add(mid); u=m['usage']; cc=u.get('cache_creation') or {}
    ts=datetime.datetime.fromisoformat(d['timestamp'].replace('Z','+00:00'))
    gap=(ts-prev).total_seconds() if prev else 0; prev=ts
    print(f"gap {gap:6.0f}s  in {u['input_tokens']:4d} read {u['cache_read_input_tokens']:6d} write {u['cache_creation_input_tokens']:6d} (5m {cc.get('ephemeral_5m_input_tokens',0)} 1h {cc.get('ephemeral_1h_input_tokens',0)}) out {u['output_tokens']}")
EOF
done

Assistant2026-08-31T00:47:27.767Z
Thinking

The 5m capture reveals a real WP-05 limitation: partial-invalidation gaps (where a stable prefix stays warm) aren't recovered by the lengthening rule since it only triggers on reads == 0, causing it to under-sell 1h on 5m-configured sessions — I'll document this as a finding rather than fix the simulator here. Now updating the manifest/README/PLAN, emitting goldens, syncing samples, and running the full check.

Assistant2026-08-31T00:47:42.076Z
$ Bash
Update docs, emit goldens, sync samples, run every check
cd ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer-wp06 && python3 - <<'PYEOF'
import json
from pathlib import Path
# manifest
p = Path('fixtures/fixtures.json'); m = json.loads(p.read_text())
for e in m['fixtures']:
    if e['path'] == 'captured/scenarios':
        e['expect'] = ("tight-loop-5m: observed 5m, 5m wins, no expiries; gap-heavy-1h: observed 1h, the 5m scenario adds three expiries, 1h wins ($0.32 vs $0.70); "
                       "gap-heavy-5m: observed 5m, one full lapse and two PARTIAL lapses (an ~11.8k stable prefix stayed warm) — the all-or-nothing model restores only the full one, so 5m wins narrowly ($0.562 vs $0.579); see fixtures/README.md; "
                       "model-switch: observed 1h, two hard resets (model, effort)")
p.write_text(json.dumps(m, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False) + '\n')

# fixtures/README captured table + finding
p = Path('fixtures/README.md'); s = p.read_text()
old = "| `captured/parallel-subagents/` |"
i = s.index(old); j = s.index('\n', i)
rows = s[i:j] + """
| `captured/scenarios/tight-loop-5m/` | Claude-enacted: eight back-to-back prompts on a toy project with `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=5m` (13 requests, largest gap 18s). | The 5m-configured capture the plan asked for. 5m wins ($0.406 vs $0.473); no expiries in either scenario. |
| `captured/scenarios/gap-heavy-1h/` | Four prompts at 1h separated by 6m / 8m / 12m of idle time (9 requests). | The shortening direction on real data: the 5m scenario adds three expiries; 1h wins ($0.32 vs $0.70). |
| `captured/scenarios/gap-heavy-5m/` | The same prompts and gaps with the TTL set to 5m (8 requests). | The lapse-heavy path on real data — and a **partial-invalidation** finding, below. 5m wins narrowly ($0.562 vs $0.579). |
| `captured/scenarios/model-switch/` | Two turns on Opus 5, `/model` to Sonnet 5, then effort high → medium, all at 1h (10 requests). | Two hard resets (`model-change`, `effort-change`) that no elapsed time explains; per-request pricing across two models. |

The scenarios were recorded by `scripts/capture-scenarios.sh`, which drives
`claude -p` in one throwaway directory per scenario (the cache is scoped to
machine + directory, so scenarios must not share one) with read-only tools,
then scrubbed like any other capture. They double as the app's bundled
samples: `scripts/sync-samples.ts` copies them to `public/samples/` and
derives `index.json` (title, description, expected verdict) from each
capture's `capture.json` and golden, and the harness asserts the copies are
byte-identical.

### Finding: partial invalidation, live

`gap-heavy-5m` shows what feasibility §7 predicted but no corpus session had
exhibited. After the first long gap (364s) the log has `read 0` and a full
re-write (25,469 tokens) — the all-or-nothing lapse. After the next two gaps
(487s, 730s) it has `read 11,808` *and* a ~14k re-write: a stable prefix
breakpoint stayed warm while the conversation tail lapsed. The WP-05
lengthening rule only restores a re-write when the request read nothing, so
the 1h counterfactual recovers the full lapse but not the two partial ones —
and 1h loses a session that, by the 1h run's numbers, it should win. That is
a modeling limit to revisit in the simulator (restore `min(write, warm −
read)` on partial lapses, or similar); until then the tool under-sells 1h on
5m-configured sessions with partial lapses, which is the opposite direction
from the §7 conservatism disclosed in the UI. The golden pins today's
behavior so the change, when made, is visible."""
s = s[:i] + rows + s[j:]
p.write_text(s)

## Summary

WP-06 — fixtures and golden data (docs/PLAN.md §4 WP-06, §5).

- **`tools/refsim/`** — the feasibility prototype rewritten to full spec parity as an independently written Python reference implementation (parser, pricing, counterfactual replay), with 29 hand-computed `unittest` cases. It emits the goldens; it is not the oracle (D7).
- **`fixtures/`** — 22 synthetic sessions (one correctness-rule trap each) + 6 adversarial inputs, generated by `scripts/build-synthetic-fixtures.ts`; real captures scrubbed to metadata by `scripts/scrub-capture.py` (D17): a v2.1.251 session with **27 parallel + nested subagent transcripts**, and four **Claude-enacted scenarios** recorded by `scripts/capture-scenarios.sh` (tight loop at 5m, gap-heavy at 1h and at 5m, model + effort switch); 60 goldens; a manifest with the trap and expectation per fixture.
- **`public/samples/`** — the four scenarios, bundled by `scripts/sync-samples.ts` with an `index.json` whose expected verdicts come from the goldens (for WP-07's sample loader).
- **`src/engine/golden.test.ts`** — runs the engine over every fixture and diffs against its golden (float tolerance 1e-10), plus the format-drift canary (one exempt fixture), the content-poison property, the reconciliation property, manifest completeness, and sample/fixture byte-identity.
- **CI** — a Python step runs the sim's tests and `refsim.py check`, so goldens cannot drift from the sim.
- PLAN.md: WP-05/WP-06 marked complete, D17/D18 added, WP-04 `inference_geo` gap resolved by stated assumption.

## Findings worth a look

1. **Partial invalidation, live** (`captured/scenarios/gap-heavy-5m`). At 5m, the first 6m gap lapsed everything (`read 0`, 25k re-write); the 8m and 12m gaps lapsed only the tail — an ~11.8k stable prefix stayed warm. The WP-05 lengthening rule restores a re-write only when `reads == 0`, so the 1h counterfactual recovers the full lapse but not the partial ones, and 1h loses ($0.562 vs $0.579) a session the 1h run shows it should win ($0.32 vs $0.70). Documented as a simulator follow-up in fixtures/README.md and PLAN.md; **the simulator is unchanged here** — that's a modeling decision.
2. **Reconciliation** (PLAN §5) holds exactly for every bucket except a 5m-dominant bucket that also carries 1h writes (a config flip): the "5m" scenario reprices the flipped writes by design. `synthetic/mixed-ttl-5m-dominant-flip` pins it; hand check 1100 × ($10 − $6.25)/MTok = $0.004125, exactly the delta.
3. `inference_geo: "us"` priced at standard rates by stated assumption (D18); `synthetic/inference-geo-us` pins it.
4. First cross-validation run agreed on 225/227 cases; the two misses were a refsim clamp bug (code points vs UTF-16 units) and finding 2.

=== samples.ts
/**
 * The bundled sample sessions offered on the landing page. WP-06 captures the
 * real ones, scrubs them, and drops the files into `public/samples/`; adding a
 * row here is all the UI needs — the "Or start with a captured session"
 * region renders only when this list has entries, so the shell ships honestly
 * empty until then.
 *
 * A sample is fetched from our own origin (`connect-src 'self'` permits that
 * and nothing else) and handed to the same worker as an uploaded file, so
 * samples exercise exactly the path a user's own log takes.
 */

import type { en } from '../i18n/en'

/** Which of the three lessons the sample was captured to teach. */
export type SampleLesson = 'five-minute-wins' | 'one-hour-wins' | 'hard-resets'

/** A sample's display name is catalog copy, so it is a checked i18n key. */
export type SampleNameKey = `samples.names.${keyof typeof en.samples.names}`

export interface SampleSession {
  /** Stable id, used as the React key and in the sample's URL. */
  id: string
  /** File name under `public/samples/`. */
  file: string
  nameKey: SampleNameKey
  lesson: SampleLesson
  /** Headline numbers shown on the card, from the captured session. */
  requestCount: number
  spanMs: number
  cacheHitRate: number
  /** Only meaningful for the `hard-resets` lesson. */
  hardResets?: number
}

export const SAMPLES: readonly SampleSession[] = []

export const SAMPLES_BASE_PATH = '/samples/'

## Repo map

| Path | What's in it |
|---|---|
| `docs/PLAN.md` | Build plan and phases |
| `docs/design/` | Claude Design exports, screenshots |
| `transcripts/` | The AI sessions that built this project ([map](transcripts/README.md)) |
| `src/` | Application source |
| `public/samples/` | Bundled demo session logs so the tool works without your own data |
| `.coderabbit.yaml` | [CodeRabbit](https://coderabbit.ai) AI review settings for pull requests |

## Development

Prerequisites: [Node.js](https://nodejs.org/) 22+ (developed on 26) and npm.
Python 3.10+ (standard library only) for the reference simulator and golden
fixtures — not needed to build or run the app.

```sh
npm  install
npm  run  dev  # Vite dev server with HMR
```

| Script | What it does |
|---|---|
| `npm run dev` | Vite dev server |
| `npm run build` | Typecheck (`tsc -b`) + production build to `dist/` |
| `npm test` | Vitest, single run (`npm run test:watch` for watch mode) |
| `npm run lint` | [Oxlint](https://oxc.rs/docs/guide/usage/linter.html) |
| `npm run format` | Prettier, write mode (`format:check` in CI) |
| `npm run typecheck` | `tsc -b` only |
| `npm run test:refsim` | Hand-computed unit tests for the Python reference simulator (`tools/refsim/`) |
| `npm run golden:emit` / `golden:check` | Regenerate / verify the golden fixtures the engine is cross-validated against
| `npm run fixtures:build` | Regenerate the synthetic fixture sessions from `scripts/build-synthetic-fixtures.ts` |
| `npm run preview` | Serve the production build via Vite |
| `npx wrangler dev` | Serve the production build the way Cloudflare Workers will (build first) |
| `npm run deploy` | Build and deploy to Cloudflare Workers (manual escape hatch; CI deploys `main` automatically) |

can never drift from the sim. [fixtures/README.md](fixtures/README.md) has
the details and the add-a-fixture recipe.

# --- sync-samples.ts rewrite
Path('scripts/sync-samples.ts').write_text('''/**
 * WP-06 — publish the scenario captures the UI lists as bundled samples.
 *
 * `src/config/samples.ts` (WP-07) is the source of truth for WHICH captures
 * ship and what their cards say; this script copies each listed sample's
 * `fixtures/captured/scenarios/<id>/session.jsonl` to `public/samples/<file>`
 * and removes anything else there. `src/engine/golden.test.ts` asserts the
 * copies are byte-identical and that every number on a card matches the
 * fixture and its golden.
 *
 * Run: `node scripts/sync-samples.ts` (also `npm run samples:sync`).
 */

import { copyFileSync, mkdirSync, readdirSync, rmSync } from 'node:fs'
import { join } from 'node:path'
import { SAMPLES } from '../src/config/samples.ts'

export const SCENARIOS_DIR = join('fixtures', 'captured', 'scenarios')
export const SAMPLES_DIR = join('public', 'samples')

export function sampleSource(id: string): string {
  return join(SCENARIOS_DIR, id, 'session.jsonl')
}

function main(): void {
  mkdirSync(SAMPLES_DIR, { recursive: true })
  const wanted = new Set(SAMPLES.map((s) => s.file))
  for (const name of readdirSync(SAMPLES_DIR)) {
    if (name.endsWith('.jsonl') && !wanted.has(name)) {
      rmSync(join(SAMPLES_DIR, name))
      console.log(`removed ${join(SAMPLES_DIR, name)}`)
    }
  }
  for (const sample of SAMPLES) {
    copyFileSync(sampleSource(sample.id), join(SAMPLES_DIR, sample.file))
    console.log(`copied ${sampleSource(sample.id)} -> ${join(SAMPLES_DIR, sample.file)}`)
  }
}

// Only run when executed directly, so the test can import the helpers.
if (process.argv[1]?.endsWith('sync-samples.ts')) main()
''')

# --- harness samples test
p = Path('src/engine/golden.test.ts'); s = p.read_text()

i = s.index("describe('bundled samples (public/samples)'")
s = s[:i] + """describe('bundled samples (public/samples)', () => {
  // The scenario captures double as the app's samples (PLAN WP-06). The UI's

    for (const sample of SAMPLES) {
      expect(readFileSync(join(SAMPLES_DIR, sample.file))).toEqual(readFileSync(sampleSource(sample.id)))
    }
    const shipped = readdirSync(SAMPLES_DIR)
      .filter((name) => name.endsWith('.jsonl'))
      .sort()
    expect(shipped).toEqual(SAMPLES.map((s) => s.file).sort())
  })

  it.each(SAMPLES.map((s) => [s.id, s] as const))('%s card numbers come from the data', async (_id, sample) => {
    const { parsed, result } = await runEngine(sampleSource(sample.id))
    expect(result).toBeDefined()
    const main = result!.buckets.find((b) => b.bucket === 'main')!
    expect(sample.requestCount).toBe(main.requestCount)
    expect(sample.spanMs).toBe(main.shape.spanMs)
    expect(sample.hardResets ?? 0).toBe(main.scenarios.oneHour.hardResets)
    let input = 0
    let read = 0
    let write = 0
    for (const r of parsed.requests) {
      input += r.usage.inputTokens
      read += r.usage.cacheReadInputTokens
      write += r.usage.cacheCreationInputTokens
    }
    expect(sample.cacheHitRate).toBeCloseTo(read / (input + read + write), 3)
    const lesson =
      main.scenarios.oneHour.hardResets > 0
        ? 'hard-resets'
        : main.recommendation === '5m'
          ? 'five-minute-wins'
          : 'one-hour-wins'
    expect(sample.lesson).toBe(lesson)
  })
})
"""
p.write_text(s)

Tool reply2026-08-31T00:51:22.622Z
Launching skill: publish-transcript
User2026-08-31T00:51:22.620Z

Base directory for this skill: ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer/.claude/skills/publish-transcript

Publish a session transcript

Turns one Claude Code session for this repo into one committed folder holding both published forms:

transcripts/<NNN-slug>/
  session.jsonl    the redacted session log
  index.html       rendered via simonw/claude-code-transcripts
  page-001.html

The HTML is rendered from the redacted JSONL, never from the original. Redact once at the source and both outputs stay consistent — never redact the rendered HTML separately.

to_text.py renders a session as plain text for your review in step 2. That rendering is a working file: it is never committed. Only session.jsonl and the rendered HTML go into the repo.

Prerequisites

claude-code-transcripts must be on PATH. If which claude-code-transcripts comes up empty, run it via uvx claude-code-transcripts instead, or install with uv tool install claude-code-transcripts.

Scripts live in .claude/skills/publish-transcript/scripts/. Below, $S means that directory and $W a scratch working directory (use the session scratchpad, not the repo — intermediate files must never be committed).

Step 1 — Pick the session

python3 $S/list_sessions.py --limit 20

Show the list and ask the user which session to publish, unless they already named one (by session id, by "the current session", or by describing its topic clearly enough to match exactly one row).

Publishing the current session: the JSONL is still being written, so it will not contain this final turn. That is expected and fine — say so, and offer to re-run later if they want the tail included. Note that the skill is loaded at session start, so a session that created or edited the skill cannot invoke it by name; follow these steps directly instead.

Step 2 — Stage and render for review

mkdir -p $W
cp ~/.claude/projects/<project-slug>/<session-id>.jsonl $W/original.jsonl
python3 $S/to_text.py $W/original.jsonl -o $W/review.txt --max-tool-output 4000

list_sessions.py prints the absolute source path — copy it from there rather than reconstructing it.

Step 3 — Find sensitive material

Two passes, both required. The regex pass catches well-shaped secrets; the reading pass catches everything shaped like ordinary prose.

Pass A — regex triage:

python3 $S/scan_secrets.py $W/original.jsonl

Pass B — read the transcript. Read $W/review.txt in full. For a long session, read it in chunks — do not skim, and do not delegate this to a subagent that only reports back a summary. You are looking for things a regex cannot match:

  • Credentials, tokens, API keys, passwords, connection strings — including ones pasted mid-sentence or shown in command output
  • Real names, emails, phone numbers, addresses of anyone (the user included)
  • Customer, client, or employer names the user may not want public
  • Internal hostnames, private URLs, ticket IDs, Slack/DM links, S3 buckets
  • Names of the user's other projects. A session that ran ls ~/.claude/projects, git remote -v elsewhere, or any directory listing above the repo will name unrelated work. A regex cannot catch this — it is the single most likely real leak in an otherwise clean session.
  • Unreleased or confidential product details discussed in passing
  • .env file contents, private keys, or ~/.aws/credentials echoed by a tool
  • Anything in a file the repo's .gitignore excludes (this repo ignores .env, .env.*) — if its contents appear in the transcript, redact them

Judgment calls to raise with the user rather than decide alone:

  • The user's own name and email. They are already in the git history of this repo, so redacting them is usually pointless — but ask, don't assume.
  • Home directory paths (/Users/<name>/...). Low risk, but they leak a username and local layout. Offer to rewrite them to ~/.
  • Third parties' names. Default to redacting these; a person who was mentioned in a session did not consent to being published.

Step 4 — Propose redactions

Write $W/redactions.json — a list of rules, each with a reason:

[
  {"find": "sk-ant-api03-REAL-KEY", "replace": "[REDACTED_API_KEY]", "reason": "Anthropic API key in tool output"},
  {"find": "coworker@example.com", "replace": "[REDACTED_EMAIL]", "reason": "third party PII"},
  {"find": "~", "replace": "~", "reason": "local home path"}
]

Rules are literal by default; add "regex": true for a pattern. Use [REDACTED_<KIND>] placeholders so a reader can tell what was removed.

Redact the shortest distinctive token, not the full prefixed value. A transcript contains truncated copies of its own content — tool output gets cut off, scanner context windows clip mid-value, and prose refers to values with an ellipsis. A rule for sk-ant-api03-<key> will sail past ...-api03-<key> and sk-ant-api03-<pre>..., leaving fragments behind. Add a rule for the bare core (<key>) as well as the full value, and for a multi-part name like acme-widget-service add one for acme too.

Watch for self-reference. Once a session discusses its own redaction, the sensitive strings appear in the discussion — in your grep commands, your findings summary, and your questions to the user. Re-copying the source JSONL after that point pulls those in, and each re-copy adds more. Copy the source once, redact and verify against that snapshot, and stop; do not chase the tail.

Present the proposed list to the user — grouped by category, with the reason and match count for each — and get approval before applying. If you find a live credential, tell the user plainly that it should be rotated, since it existed in plaintext on disk regardless of what gets published.

If both passes come up clean, say so explicitly and confirm they want to publish unredacted rather than silently proceeding.

Step 5 — Apply and verify

python3 $S/apply_redactions.py $W/original.jsonl $W/redactions.json \
    -o $W/redacted.jsonl --verify
python3 $S/scan_secrets.py $W/redacted.jsonl

--verify fails if any literal target survived. Investigate a NO MATCHES warning before continuing — it usually means a typo'd rule, which means something you meant to redact is still in the file.

The second scan is the real check: re-read its output and confirm every remaining hit is something you and the user consciously decided to keep.

Step 6 — Render the two artifacts

Name the session NNN-short-topic: a zero-padded counter one higher than the highest folder already in transcripts/, plus a kebab-case topic — e.g. 002-cost-model.

--json copies the JSONL into the output directory alongside the HTML, naming it after the input file. Rename the redacted file to session.jsonl first so it lands with the right name, and the whole folder is produced in one command:

SLUG=<NNN-short-topic>
mv $W/redacted.jsonl $W/session.jsonl
claude-code-transcripts json $W/session.jsonl -o transcripts/$SLUG --json

Do not pass --gist — that uploads to a public Gist, which is a separate publishing decision the user has to make explicitly.

Then confirm the redaction survived rendering:

python3 $S/scan_secrets.py transcripts/$SLUG/*

Step 7 — Update the session map

Add a row to the table in transcripts/README.md:

Date Session Covers Share link Notes
2026-08-30 001-transcript-skill Built the transcript publishing skill open 3 emails redacted

Keep rows in date order. Put a short, honest note about what was redacted in the Notes column — that is the reader's signal that gaps are deliberate.

The share link is the GitHub Pages copy — https://cebert.github.io/cache-ttl-analyzer/<slug>/, live once the commit reaches main. This table is also the source for that site's landing page, so a missing or misspelled row fails the Transcripts site workflow rather than publishing a broken index. Check the table still matches the folders with:

npm run build:transcripts

Ask the user what the session covers — don't just write it yourself. Propose a one-line summary drawn from reading the transcript, and ask them to confirm or replace it. They know why the session mattered; the transcript only shows what happened in it.

Aim the summary at why the work was done, not the mechanics of what was typed. Where a session advanced a stated goal of the project, say so — that connection is the reason the transcript is worth keeping, and it is exactly the part a reader cannot reconstruct from the log.

  • Weak: "Created four Python scripts and a SKILL.md"
  • Better: "Built the transcript publishing skill, so build sessions can be published without leaking secrets or PII — a stated goal of the project"

Use the same confirmed summary for the commit subject in step 8, so the map row and the git history agree.

Step 8 — Commit

This repo uses Conventional Commits. Transcript publishing is a docs change:

git add transcripts/
git commit -m "docs(transcripts): add session log for <topic>"

Guidance:

  • Subject in imperative mood, no trailing period, under ~72 characters.
  • If anything was redacted, note it in the body — e.g. Redacted 2 API keys and 3 third-party email addresses before publishing.
  • Commit only under transcripts/; leave unrelated working-tree changes alone.
  • Do not push unless the user asks. Publishing to a remote is their call, and it is the point of no return for anything that slipped through redaction.

184	  # Build plan
185	  
186	  The plan for taking cache-ttl-analyzer from scaffolding to a deployed MVP.
187	  Grounded in the research at
188	  [docs/design/claude-code-session-logs/feasibility.md](design/claude-code-session-logs/feasibility.md)
189	  (referenced below as "the feasibility doc") — read it before implementing the
190	  parser or simulator; it documents the traps that make naive implementations
191	  silently wrong.
192	  
193	  This plan is decomposed into **work packages (WPs)** sized for individual Claude
194	  sessions, with an explicit dependency graph so independent WPs can run in
195	  parallel.
196	  
197	  ---
198	  
199	  ## 1. MVP definition
200	  
201	  A static web app, deployed to Cloudflare, where a user:
202	  
203	  1. Uploads **one Claude Code session log (JSONL)** at a time — or loads a
204	     bundled sample.
205	  2. Watches analysis run **entirely in their browser** (Web Worker), with a
206	     progress indicator and a cancel button.
207	  3. Gets a verdict: what the session actually cost at published Anthropic API
208	     rates, what it *would have cost* under the other cache TTL (5m vs 1h), and a
209	     recommendation.
210	  4. Sees cache-behavior insights: cache invalidations (expiry gaps, and hard
211	     resets from model / effort / version changes), warm-read counts, and short
212	     educational explanations of each.
213	  5. Can revisit sessions analyzed earlier in the same browser session
214	     (in-memory history only — nothing persisted).
215	  
216	  Plus: clear instructions for finding session logs on macOS and Windows, a
217	  prominent privacy statement ("analysis is local; this is open source — verify
218	  it yourself" with a link to the repo), a dedicated **data policy page** (see
219	  §3a), and a "prices as of" date on every dollar figure.
220	  
221	  Two cross-cutting requirements, cheap now and expensive to retrofit:
222	  
223	  - **Localization-ready from day one.** MVP ships English only, but no
224	    user-facing string is hard-coded in a component: all copy lives in locale
225	    resource files (react-i18next or equivalent — chosen in WP-07), and all
226	    number, currency, date, and duration formatting goes through the `Intl`
227	  ...
228	  </persisted-output>

250	  17	
251	  18	A static web app, deployed to Cloudflare, where a user:
252	  19	
253	  20	1. Uploads **one Claude Code session log (JSONL)** at a time — or loads a
254	  21	   bundled sample.
255	  22	2. Watches analysis run **entirely in their browser** (Web Worker), with a
256	  23	   progress indicator and a cancel button.
257	  24	3. Gets a verdict: what the session actually cost at published Anthropic API
258	  25	   rates, what it *would have cost* under the other cache TTL (5m vs 1h), and a
259	  26	   recommendation.
260	  27	4. Sees cache-behavior insights: cache invalidations (expiry gaps, and hard
261	  28	   resets from model / effort / version changes), warm-read counts, and short
262	  29	   educational explanations of each.
263	  30	5. Can revisit sessions analyzed earlier in the same browser session
264	  31	   (in-memory history only — nothing persisted).
265	  32	
266	  33	Plus: clear instructions for finding session logs on macOS and Windows, a
267	  34	prominent privacy statement ("analysis is local; this is open source — verify
268	  35	it yourself" with a link to the repo), a dedicated **data policy page** (see
269	  36	§3a), and a "prices as of" date on every dollar figure.
270	  37	
271	  38	Two cross-cutting requirements, cheap now and expensive to retrofit:
272	  39	
273	  40	- **Localization-ready from day one.** MVP ships English only, but no
274	  41	  user-facing string is hard-coded in a component: all copy lives in locale
275	  42	  resource files (react-i18next or equivalent — chosen in WP-07), and all
276	  43	  number, currency, date, and duration formatting goes through the `Intl`
277	  44	  APIs keyed to the active locale. Adding a second language later must be a
278	  45	  translation task, not a refactor.
279	  46	- **Data policy page**: a first-class page, linked from the footer and the
280	  47	  upload screen, stating in plain language: analysis runs entirely in the
281	  48	  browser; the file is never uploaded, stored, or sent anywhere; the strict
282	  49	  CSP enforces this at the platform level (with a one-line "check devtools"
283	  50	  pointer); no analytics or tracking in the MVP; the project is open source
284	  51	  (repo link); and any future optional API-powered analysis will be opt-in
285	  52	  with an explicit, itemized disclosure of what would be sent.
286	  53	
287	  54	### Non-goals for MVP (deferred, in rough priority order)
288	  55	
289	  56	- **Anthropic API deep-analysis feature** (upload timestamps/token counts for
290	  57	  richer analysis). Deferred until MVP proves useful. When picked up, the
291	  58	  BYO-API-key vs. our-key-plus-rate-limiting decision gets made then.
292	  59	- Multi-file / folder upload and cross-session rollups (requires global dedup
293	  60	  and cross-session cache modeling — feasibility doc §6.3, §7).
294	  61	- Provider-specific rate tables (Bedrock, Vertex). MVP uses Anthropic
295	  62	  published API rates only — see decision log.
296	  63	- Claude Code **web JSON export** input. Unverified that it carries `usage`
297	  64	  (feasibility doc §10). JSONL only for MVP.
298	  65	- Durable persistence, accounts, authentication.
299	  66	- In-app billing-mode questionnaire (feasibility §3 suggests asking the user;
300	  67	  MVP states the API-rates framing as a label instead — see D12).
301	  68	- Hybrid-TTL guidance (1h stable prefix + 5m tail, feasibility §10) — the
302	  69	  logs can't support a computed number, only qualitative advice; revisit
303	  70	  post-MVP.

360	   *     are main-thread unless `isSidechain` is true (legacy interleaved
361	   *     logs; threads recovered from `parentUuid` chain roots). A modern
362	   *     main-session upload contains no subagent traffic.
363	   * F3. Request start: walking `parentUuid` reaches a `user` row for 100% of
364	   *     requests (the immediate parent is usually an `attachment`, 77/79);
365	   *     that timestamp precedes the assistant row's by 1.8s min / 3.9s median
366	   *     / 26s p90 / 76s max, never negative. Rule: nearest `user`-row
367	   *     ancestor, else the assistant row's own timestamp. Requires indexing
368	   *     uuid/parentUuid/timestamp of `user` and `attachment` rows — metadata
369	   *     only, never content.
370	   * F4. Record types beyond the feasibility doc's list already exist
371	   *     (frame-link, pr-link, artifact-comment-monitor,
372	   *     artifact-autoreact-ledger) — skip-and-count is load-bearing today.
373	   * F5. Usage subfields are optional: subagent rows lacked
374	   *     output_tokens_details, server_tool_use, iterations, and speed.
375	   *     `service_tier`/`speed` default to "standard"; unknown additions
376	   *     (e.g. inference_geo) are ignored.
377	   * F6. `parentUuid` is not linear across a message's content-block rows
378	   *     (56/122 continuation rows chained). Dedup by `message.id` only.
379	   * F7. `message.id` <-> `requestId` stayed 1:1 (79:79) — dedup key holds.
380	   */
381	  
382	  /** A cache TTL the user can configure per bucket. */
383	  export type CacheTtl = '5m' | '1h'
384	  
385	  export const CACHE_TTL_5M_MS = 5 * 60_000
386	  export const CACHE_TTL_1H_MS = 60 * 60_000
387

405	  /**
406	   * WP-05 — the counterfactual simulator: replays a parsed session under
407	   * each cache TTL and produces the per-bucket `AnalysisResult` the UI
408	   * consumes (docs/PLAN.md §3, feasibility doc §6–§7).
409	   *
410	   * MODEL. Requests are partitioned into the two buckets the settings govern
411	   * ("main" = `promptCacheTtl`, "subagent" = `subagentPromptCacheTtl`) and,
412	   * inside a bucket, into cache threads (each subagent has its own cache).
413	   * Within a thread, requests are replayed in file order and the cache entry
414	   * is alive at request r iff the same-thread gap since the previous request
415	   * START is within the TTL (reads refresh the timer at no cost, so the clock
416	   * restarts at every request).
417	   *
418	   * The scenario at the OBSERVED TTL reproduces the log exactly (the
419	   * reconciliation property, PLAN §5). A counterfactual only edits the
420	   * requests where the two TTL windows disagree, all-or-nothing (§7):
421	   *  - shortening (observed 1h, scenario 5m): where 5m < gap and the log shows
422	   *    a cache read, that read lapses and becomes a fresh write at the 5m
423	   *    rate ("expiry");
424	   *  - lengthening (observed 5m, scenario 1h): where 5m < gap <= 1h and the
425	   *    log shows no read but a write, the lapsed prefix (bounded by what was
426	   *    cached after the previous request) would have been read instead.
427	   * A change of model, effort, or version between consecutive same-thread
428	   * requests is a hard reset in every scenario (§6.6): the cache is emptied,
429	   * no expiry is attributed to that gap, and the observed usage stands.
430	   *
431	   * MIXED TTLs (contract, frozen): only the user-controllable write share is
432	   * repriced. Server tools only ever add 5m writes, so in a 1h-dominant
433	   * bucket the 5m residual is the server-tool share — kept at 5m in both
434	   * scenarios as its own expiry class and never simulated for expiry (its
435	   * reads are not separable from `cache_read_input_tokens`). In a
436	   * 5m-dominant bucket every write is user-controlled, including a 1h
437	   * residual (a mid-session config flip), per the contract's rationale that
438	   * nonzero 1h tokens are user-controlled. The warm entry's observed TTL is
439	   * the bucket's dominant TTL: a request whose only write was a server-tool
440	   * 5m write did not shorten the user's live entry.
441	   *
442	   * UNKNOWN MODELS (contract, frozen): excluded from every dollar figure but
443	   * still replayed so cache state and events stay right; a bucket whose
444	   * unpriced token share exceeds `UNKNOWN_MODEL_SUPPRESSION_RATIO` gets
445	   * "no-verdict".
446	   */
447

496	      if (!list) {
497	        list = []
498	        threads.set(request.threadId, list)
499	      }

510	  /**
511	   * WP-04 — cost engine: prices token buckets at published Anthropic API
512	   * rates (decision D2) using a `PricingConfig` (`pricing.ts`).
513	   *
514	   * Pure functions, no config file dependency: the worker receives the
515	   * pricing over the protocol. Two entry points matter to callers:
516	   *  - `priceRequest` prices what actually happened (`RequestRecord`).
517	   *  - `priceTokens` prices arbitrary buckets, so the simulator (WP-05) can
518	   *    reprice a request under a counterfactual TTL with the same modifiers.
519	   *
520	   * Rate model (pricing page, "Feature-specific pricing"): the `service_tier`
521	   * and `speed` multipliers scale both the input-side and output rates;
522	   * cache multipliers then apply to the (scaled) input rate. Missing
523	   * multiplier keys mean 1.0 — unknown *models* are never guessed (contract
524	   * degradation policy); unknown *tiers/speeds* are priced at standard.
525	   */
526	  
527	  import type { CostBreakdown, RequestRecord, RequestUsage, UnknownModelReport } from './contract'
528	  import type { CacheMultipliers, ModelPricing, PricingConfig } from './pricing'
529	  
530	  const TOKENS_PER_MTOK = 1_000_000
531	  
532	  /** Token buckets a price is computed from — the unit the simulator reprices. */
533	  export interface TokenBuckets {
534	    baseInputTokens: number
535	    cacheReadTokens: number
536	    cacheWrite5mTokens: number
537	    cacheWrite1hTokens: number
538	    outputTokens: number
539	  }
540	  
541	  export interface RateModifiers {
542	    serviceTier: string
543	    speed: string
544	  }
545	  
546	  export const ZERO_COST: CostBreakdown = Object.freeze({
547	    baseInputUsd: 0,
548	    cacheReadUsd: 0,
549	    cacheWrite5mUsd: 0,
550	    cacheWrite1hUsd: 0,
551	    outputUsd: 0,
552	    totalUsd: 0,
553	  })
554	  
555	  /** Multiplier lookup with the schema's defaulting rule: missing = 1.0. */
556	  export function multiplierFor(map: Record<string, number> | undefined, key: string): number {
557	    if (!map || !Object.hasOwn(map, key)) return 1
558	    return map[key]
559	  }
560	  
561	  /** Price token buckets for one model under the given modifiers. */
562	  export function priceTokens(
563	    buckets: TokenBuckets,
564	    model: ModelPricing,
565	    cache: CacheMultipliers,
566	    modifiers: RateModifiers,
567	  ): CostBreakdown {
568	    const scale =
569	      multiplierFor(model.serviceTierMultipliers, modifiers.serviceTier) *
570	      multiplierFor(model.speedMultipliers, modifiers.speed)
571	    const inputRate = model.inputPerMTok * scale
572	    const outputRate = model.outputPerMTok * scale
573	    const perToken = (tokens: number, rate: number) => (tokens / TOKENS_PER_MTOK) * rate
574	  
575	    const baseInputUsd = perToken(buckets.baseInputTokens, inputRate)
576	    const cacheReadUsd = perToken(buckets.cacheReadTokens, inputRate * cache.read)
577	    const cacheWrite5mUsd = perToken(buckets.cacheWrite5mTokens, inputRate * cache.write5m)
578	    const cacheWrite1hUsd = perToken(buckets.cacheWrite1hTokens, inputRate * cache.write1h)
579	    const outputUsd = perToken(buckets.outputTokens, outputRate)
580	    return {
581	      baseInputUsd,
582	      cacheReadUsd,
583	      cacheWrite5mUsd,
584	      cacheWrite1hUsd,
585	      outputUsd,
586	      totalUsd: baseInputUsd + cacheReadUsd + cacheWrite5mUsd + cacheWrite1hUsd + outputUsd,
587	    }
588	  }
589	  
590	  /**
591	   * Cache-write tokens the `cache_creation` split does not account for
592	   * (total exceeds 5m + 1h — e.g. a log written before the split existed).
593	   */
594	  export function unattributedWriteTokens(usage: RequestUsage): number {
595	    return Math.max(
596	      0,
597	      usage.cacheCreationInputTokens - usage.cacheCreation5mTokens - usage.cacheCreation1hTokens,
598	    )
599	  }
600	  
601	  /**
602	   * Observed usage as token buckets. Unattributed write tokens are priced at
603	   * the 5m rate — the API's default TTL when none is requested — rather than
604	   * dropped, so actual cost never understates the bill.
605	   */
606	  export function bucketsFromUsage(usage: RequestUsage): TokenBuckets {
607	    return {
608	      baseInputTokens: usage.inputTokens,
609	      cacheReadTokens: usage.cacheReadInputTokens,
610	      cacheWrite5mTokens: usage.cacheCreation5mTokens + unattributedWriteTokens(usage),
611	      cacheWrite1hTokens: usage.cacheCreation1hTokens,
612	      outputTokens: usage.outputTokens,
613	    }
614	  }

617	  export function totalTokens(usage: RequestUsage):

709	  # Session transcripts
710	  
711	  A goal of this project is to keep a record of the AI sessions that helped build this tool. Claude Code sessions uploaded to this repository using the [Claude Code Transcripts](https://github.com/simonw/claude-code-transcripts?tab=readme-ov-file) tool created by Simon Willison.
712	  
713	  Each session lives in its own folder, named with a zero-padded counter and a
714	  short topic slug, holding both published forms:
715	  
716	  ```
717	  transcripts/001-transcript-skill/
718	    session.jsonl    the session log, after redaction
719	    index.html       rendered via simonw/claude-code-transcripts
720	    page-001.html
721	  ```
722	  
723	  Rendering is done with [simonw/claude-code-transcripts](https://github.com/simonw/claude-code-transcripts).
724	  The table below is the index — start there rather than browsing the folders.
725	  
726	  Sessions are reviewed for secrets and PII and redacted before publishing.
727	  Removed values are replaced with `[REDACTED_...]` placeholders, and the Notes
728	  column below records what was taken out of each session.
729	  
730	  ## Publishing a session
731	  
732	  Run the `publish-transcript` skill in Claude Code — ask it to publish a session
733	  transcript, or invoke it directly. It lists the recorded sessions for this repo,
734	  reviews the one you pick for sensitive material, applies redactions you approve,
735	  renders both formats, updates the table below, and commits the result.

755	  # Feasibility: computing cache-TTL cost counterfactuals from Claude Code session logs
756	  
757	  **Date:** 2026-08-30
758	  **Question:** Do Claude Code session logs contain enough data to recompute what a
759	  session would have cost under a different `promptCacheTtl` (5m vs 1h), using
760	  Anthropic's published API rates?
761	  
762	  **Verdict: Yes — build it.** Every input the cost model needs is present per
763	  request. The exact-dollar *actual* cost is fully determined by the logs; the
764	  *counterfactual* is a simulation with one honest approximation, described below.
765	  
766	  Two things the official docs added that reshape the product, both detailed later:
767	  there are **two TTL settings**, not one (`promptCacheTtl` and
768	  `subagentPromptCacheTtl`), so the tool should report two recommendations; and the
769	  **defaults depend on how the user is billed**, which determines whether the dollar
770	  figure is their real bill or a notional at-API-rates comparison. The one standing
771	  risk is that Anthropic documents the file format as internal and unstable (§4).
772	  
773	  A working prototype (`prototype-sim.py`, alongside this doc) ran over 7 real
774	  sessions end-to-end and produced differentiated recommendations, so this is
775	  confirmed by execution, not by reading the schema.
776	  
777	  ---
778	  
779	  ## 1. Where the logs live and what they are
780	  
781	  [Officially](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sessions#where-transcripts-are-stored):
782	  `~/.claude/projects/<project>/<session-id>.jsonl`, where `<project>` is the working
783	  directory path with non-alphanumeric characters replaced by `-` (truncated to 200
784	  chars plus a hash of the full path when longer). `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` can move the
785	  root and `CLAUDE_CODE_PROJECT_DIR_NAME` can override the `<project>` segment, so an
786	  uploader must not assume the path encodes a real directory. Transcripts are cleaned
787	  up after 30 days by default (`cleanupPeriodDays`), which caps how much history any
788	  user can bring.
789	  
790	  One JSON object per line. Record `type` values observed: `assistant`, `user`, `attachment`, `system`,
791	  `file-history-snapshot`, `file-history-delta`, `mode`, `permission-mode`,
792	  `bridge-session`, `ai-title`, `atis-latch`, `last-prompt`, `queue-operation` —
793	  and the list keeps growing (§12).
794	  
795	  **Only `type: "assistant"` rows carry billing data.** But not every other row
796	  is ignorable (amended 2026-08-30 — §12): `user` and `attachment` rows carry no
797	  billing payload, yet their `uuid`/`parentUuid`/`timestamp` metadata must be
798	  retained for request-start pairing, and the last `ai-title` record feeds the
799	  session identification card. Everything else can be skipped and counted.
800	  
801	  ## 2. The billing payload
802	  
803	  Every assistant row has `message.usage`:
804	  
805	  ```json
806	  {
807	    "input_tokens": 2,
808	    "cache_creation_input_tokens": 13185,
809	    "cache_read_input_tokens": 23472,
810	    "output_tokens": 386,
811	    "output_tokens_details": { "thinking_tokens": 237 },
812	    "cache_creation": {
813	      "ephemeral_5m_input_tokens": 0,
814	      "ephemeral_1h_input_tokens": 13185
815	    },
816	    "service_tier": "standard",
817	    "speed": "standard",
818	    "server_tool_use": { "web_search_requests": 0, "web_fetch_requests": 0 },
819	    "iterations": [ ... ]
820	  }
821	  ```
822	  
823	  Plus on the row itself: `timestamp` (ISO-8601, ms), `requestId`, `message.id`,
824	  `message.model`, `isSidechain`, `sessionId`, `cwd`, `gitBranch`, `version`.
825	  
826	  This is everything the cost model needs:
827	  
828	  | Needed | Field | Notes |
829	  |---|---|---|
830	  | Which model priced it | `message.model` | e.g. `claude-opus-5`, `claude-opus-4-8` |
831	  | Uncached input | `usage.input_tokens` | |
832	  | Cache reads | `usage.cache_read_input_tokens` | billed 0.1× base input |
833	  | Cache writes | `usage.cache_creation_input_tokens` | |
834	  | **Which TTL was actually used** | `usage.cache_creation.ephemeral_5m_input_tokens` / `ephemeral_1h_input_tokens` | the decisive field |
835	  | Output | `usage.output_tokens` | thinking tokens are already included |
836	  | Request timing | `timestamp` on the assistant row; the preceding `user` row approximates request *start* | drives the expiry simulation |
837	  | Rate modifiers | `usage.service_tier`, `usage.speed` | `speed: "fast"` on Opus 5 prices a
838	  ... [7113 characters truncated]
839	
840	[tool use: Bash]
841	  command: cd ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer; sed -n 200,469p docs/design/claude-code-session-logs/feasibility.md
842	  description: Read feasibility doc part 2

844	[tool result: Bash]
845	    `message.id`, `message.model`, `message.usage`, `isSidechain`, `effort`, `version` —
846	    eight fields. Never touch `message.content`. A narrow parser survives more releases
847	    and, per §9, is also the privacy-correct design.
848	  - **Fail loudly and partially.** An unrecognized record type should be skipped and
849	    counted, not crash the run; surface "N records skipped" in the UI.
850	  - **Ship fixtures** from each validated version so a format change shows up in CI
851	    rather than in a user's browser.
852	  
853	  ## 5. The pricing math
854	  
855	  From Anthropic's published rates:
856	  
857	  - cache **read** = 0.1× the base input rate
858	  - cache **write, 5m TTL** = 1.25× base input
859	  - cache **write, 1h TTL** = 2.0× base input
860	  - A cache read **refreshes the entry's timer at no cost**, on either TTL.
861	  - Lifetime is measured from the **start** of the request that writes or reads it —
862	    generation time counts against the window.
863	  
864	  Base rates ($/1M): Opus 5 and Opus 4.8 $5/$25 · Sonnet 5 $2/$10 ·
865	  Sonnet 4.6 $3/$15 · Haiku 4.5 $1/$5 · Fable 5 $10/$50.
866	  
867	  Break-even: 5m pays for itself at 2 requests (1.25 + 0.1 = 1.35 vs 2.0 uncached);
868	  1h needs 3+ (2.0 + 0.2 = 2.2 vs 3.0). **1h only wins when gaps exceed 5 minutes** —
869	  otherwise every request refreshes the 5m entry for free and the doubled write price
870	  is pure loss. This is exactly the tradeoff the tool exists to quantify.
871	  
872	  ## 6. Traps that will silently produce wrong numbers
873	  
874	  These are the reason a naive implementation gives garbage, and they're the real
875	  value of this research.
876	  
877	  ### 6.1 One JSONL row per content block — dedup is mandatory
878	  
879	  Claude Code writes **one row per content block of an assistant message**, and
880	  **every one of those rows carries the full message `usage`**. Observed in real
881	  logs: `msg_011CdygtRmd95FeRM1AqU7Xt` appears **13 times**, all with identical
882	  `output_tokens: 12528`, `cache_read: 37044`. Summing rows overstates cost by up to
883	  13×. Across the sample, 281 rows collapsed to 142 real requests.
884	  
885	  **Dedup on `message.id`.** Verified 1:1 with `requestId` across all 669 assistant
886	  rows (0 requestIds mapping to >1 message id, and vice versa), so either key works.
887	  
888	  ### 6.2 Synthetic rows
889	  
890	  `message.model == "<synthetic>"` rows exist (2 in the sample, from API error
891	  paths). They have no `service_tier`/`speed` and represent no billable call.
892	  Exclude them.
893	  
894	  ### 6.3 Cross-file duplication
895	  
896	  `--resume` / fork can copy history into a new session file. Not observed in this
897	  sample (0 message ids in >1 file), but if the tool accepts a folder upload,
898	  dedup must be **global across files**, not per-file.
899	  
900	  ### 6.4 Sidechains are a separate cache namespace
901	  
902	  > **Amended 2026-08-30 (WP-02 inspection — see §12):** on v2.1.251 subagent
903	  > turns are *separate files*, not `isSidechain: true` rows in the session
904	  > file. The partitioning requirement stands; the mechanism changed.
905	  
906	  `isSidechain: true` marks subagent turns. A subagent starts its own conversation
907	  with its own system prompt and tool set, so its first request cannot read the
908	  parent's cache and it warms one of its own. The simulator must partition by
909	  `isSidechain` before computing gaps.
910	  
911	  Per §3 this is also the boundary of the second setting, `subagentPromptCacheTtl` —
912	  so this is not merely a correctness detail but the split along which the tool
913	  reports two separate recommendations. None appeared in the original sample,
914	  which left the path untested against real data at the time this section was
915	  written. *(Superseded: a real subagent session — in the modern separate-file
916	  layout — has since been captured and inspected; §12.)*
917	  
918	  ### 6.5 Model and rate heterogeneity within one session
919	  
920	  The corpus spans `claude-opus-5` and `claude-opus-4-8`, though each individual
921	  session held one model. Price per request anyway — a mid-session `/model` switch is
922	  both legal and, per the docs below, a full cache invalidation. Also honor
923	  `service_tier` (batch = 50%) and `speed` (fast mode = 2× on Opus 5).
924	  
925	  ### 6.6 Model, effort, and version changes reset the cache entirely
926	  
927	  Per Anthropic's docs, the cache key in
928	  ... [13706 characters truncated]
929

931	  command: cd ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer; sed -n 1,120p src/engine/simulator.test.ts; echo "=====ENGINE TEST====="; cat src/engine/engine.test.ts
932	  description: Read simulator and engine tests
933

986	  function req(o: ReqOpts): RequestRecord {
987	    const w5m = o.w5m ?? 0
988	    const w1h = o.w1h ?? 0
989	    const usage: RequestUsage = {
990	      inputTokens: o.input ?? 10,
991	      cacheReadInputTokens: o.read ?? 0,
992	      cacheCreationInputTokens: w5m + w1h,
993	      cacheCreation5mTokens: w5m,
994	      cacheCreation1hTokens: w1h,
995	      outputTokens: o.output ?? 5,
996	      serviceTier: o.tier ?? 'standard',
997	      speed: o.speed ?? 'standard',
998	    }
999	    const record: RequestRecord = {
1000	      messageId: o.id,
1001	      model: o.model ?? 'claude-opus-5',
1002	      timestamp: at(o.end ?? o.start + 3),
1003	      requestStartTimestamp: at(o.start),
1004	      requestStartSource: 'user-ancestor',
1005	      threadId: o.threadId ?? (o.sidechain ? 'agent-1' : 'main'),
1006	      isSidechain: o.sidechain ?? false,
1007	      usage,
1008	    }
1009	    if (o.effort !== undefined) record.effort = o.effort
1010	    if (o.version !== undefined) record.version = o.version
1011	    return record
1012	  }

I recommend (a).

1574	    const match = /^(\d{1,9})\.(\d{1,9})\.(\d{1,9})$/.exec(version)
1575	    if (!match) return null
1576	    return [Number(match[1]), Number(match[2]), Number(match[3])]
1577	  }

1579	  function compareVersions(a: [number, number, number], b: [number, number, number]): number {
1580	    for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) {
1581	      if (a[i] !== b[i]) return a[i] < b[i] ? -1 : 1
1582	    }
1583	    return 0
1584	  }
1585	  
1586	  /** Inclusive x.y.z comparison against `VALIDATED_VERSION_RANGE`; unparseable = out of range. */
1587	  export function isVersionInValidatedRange(version: string): boolean {
1588	    const parsed = parseVersion(version)
1589	    if (!parsed) return false
1590	    const min = parseVersion(VALIDATED_VERSION_RANGE.min)
1591	    const max = parseVersion(VALIDATED_VERSION_RANGE.max)
1592	    if (!min || !max) return false
1593	    return compareVersions(parsed, min) >= 0 && compareVersions(parsed, max) <= 0
1594	  }

1642	  /**
1643	   * WP-03 — byte stream -> JSONL lines, with the per-line length cap.
1644	   *
1645	   * The parser must never hold the whole file (or one runaway line) in
1646	   * memory (docs/PLAN.md §2, "Resource limits"). This splitter keeps only the
1647	   * bytes of the line currently being assembled; once a line exceeds
1648	   * `MAX_LINE_LENGTH_BYTES` its pending bytes are dropped and the rest of the
1649	   * line is discarded as it streams past, so peak memory is bounded by the
1650	   * cap plus one input chunk regardless of file or line size.
1651	   *
1652	   * Lines are split on `\n` (an ASCII byte, so splitting before UTF-8
1653	   * decoding is safe); a trailing `\r` and a leading BOM are removed.
1654	   */
1655	  
1656	  import { MAX_LINE_LENGTH_BYTES } from './contract'
1657

1659	  const CARRIAGE_RETURN = 0x0d
1660

1664	    | { kind: 'capped'; bytes: number }

1691	    push(chunk: Uint8Array): LineEvent[] {
1692	      const events: LineEvent[] = []
1693	      let start = 0
1694	      for (let i = 0; i < chunk.length; i++) {
1695	        if (chunk[i] !== NEWLINE) continue
1696	        this.append(chunk.subarray(start, i))
1697	        events.push(this.flushLine())

1701	      return events
1702	    }

1704	    /** Signal end of input; returns the final unterminated line, if any. */
1705	    finish(): LineEvent[] {
1706	      if (this.discarding) return [this.flushLine()]
1707	      if (this.pendingBytes === 0) return []
1708	      return [this.flushLine()]
1709	    }
1710	  
1711	    private append(segment: Uint8Array): void {
1712	      if (segment.length === 0) return
1713	      if (this.discarding) {
1714	        this.discardedBytes += segment.length
1715	        return
1716	      }
1717	      if (this.pendingBytes + segment.length > this.maxLineBytes) {
1718	        // Over the cap: drop what we have and skip to the next newline.
1719	        this.discardedBytes = this.pendingBytes + segment.length
1720	        this.pending = []
1721	        this.pendingBytes = 0
1722	        this.discarding = true
1723	        return
1724	      }
1725	      // Copy: the caller may reuse the chunk's backing buffer.
1726	      this.pending.push(segment.slice())
1727	      this.pendingBytes += segment.length
1728	      if (this.pendingBytes > this.peakPendingBytes) this.peakPendingBytes = this.pendingBytes
1729	    }

1742	      } else {
1743	        bytes = new Uint8Array(this.pendingBytes)
1744	        let offset = 0
1745	        for (const part of this.pending) {
1746	          bytes.set(part, offset)
1747	          offset += part.length

1749	      }

1754	      }

2002	        speed="standard",
2003	    ):

2060	    class PricingTests(unittest.TestCase):
2061	        def test_opus5_each_bucket(self):
2062	            # base 1000×$5 = 0.005; read 2000×$0.50 = 0.001; 5m 3000×$6.25 = 0.01875;
2063	            # 1h 4000×$10 = 0.04; out 500×$25 = 0.0125 → total 0.07725
2064	            cost = refsim.price_tokens(
2065	                {"baseInputTokens": 1000, "cacheReadTokens": 2000, "cacheWrite5mTokens": 3000, "cacheWrite1hTokens": 4000, "outputTokens": 500},
2066	                PRICING["models"]["claude-opus-5"],
2067	                PRICING["cacheMultipliers"],
2068	                "standard",
2069	                "standard",
2070	            )
2071	            self.assertAlmostEqual(cost["baseInputUsd"], 0.005, places=12)
2072	            self.assertAlmostEqual(cost["cacheReadUsd"], 0.001, places=12)
2073	            self.assertAlmostEqual(cost["cacheWrite5mUsd"], 0.01875, places=12)
2074	            self.assertAlmostEqual(cost["cacheWrite1hUsd"], 0.04, places=12)
2075	            self.assertAlmostEqual(cost["outputUsd"], 0.0125, places=12)
2076	            self.assertAlmostEqual(cost["totalUsd"], 0.07725, places=12)

2534	    #!/usr/bin/env python3
2535	    """Scrub a captured Claude Code session so it can ship as a public fixture.
2536	    
2537	    Real captures (docs/PLAN.md WP-06, kind 2) contain the whole conversation:
2538	    prompts, file contents, tool output, paths. The analyzer never reads any of
2539	    that, so the fixture does not need it either. This script keeps the exact
2540	    row structure and every field the engine consumes, and replaces EVERYTHING
2541	    else with placeholders:
2542	    
2543	    - Numbers, booleans and nulls are kept everywhere (token counts, flags).
2544	    - Strings are kept only under an allowlist of metadata keys (ids, timestamps,
2545	      model, version, effort, service tier, speed, record types...). Any other
2546	      string, at any depth, becomes the placeholder — which carries the
2547	      CONTENT_POISON marker so the golden harness proves it never leaks.
2548	    - `message.content` becomes a single placeholder block; the row-per-block
2549	      layout (one assistant row per content block) is what exercises dedup, and
2550	      that survives because rows are never merged or dropped.
2551	    - `cwd` is rewritten to `~/…` and `ai-title` gets the title you pass.
2552	    - The `agent-<id>.meta.json` sidecars are copied with their description
2553	      blanked.
2554	    
2555	    Usage:
2556	        scrub-capture.py SESSION.jsonl OUT_DIR --title "..." [--name session.jsonl]
2557	    
2558	    `OUT_DIR/session.jsonl` is written, plus `OUT_DIR/subagents/` when the
2559	    session directory has one, plus `OUT_DIR/capture.json` describing the source
2560	    (version, counts, scrub rules) for `fixtures/README.md`.

2594	    
2595	    
2596	    def scrub_value(value, key: str | None, ctx: dict):
2597	        if isinstance(value, dict):
2598	            out = {}
2599	            for k, v in value.items():
2600	                if k == "content":
2601	                    # Conversation payload on user/assistant/system/queue rows.
2602	                    if isinstance(v, list):
2603	                        out[k] = [{"type": "text", "text": PLACEHOLDER}] if v else []
2604	                    else:
2605	                        out[k] = PLACEHOLDER
2606	                    ctx["scrubbed"] += 1
2607	                    continue
2608	                if k == "cwd" and isinstance(v, str):
2609	                    out[k] = HOME_RE.sub("~", v)
2610	                    continue
2611	                if k == "aiTitle" and isinstance(v, str):
2612	                    out[k] = ctx["title"]
2613	                    continue
2614	                out[k] = scrub_value(v, k, ctx)
2615	            return out
2616	        if isinstance(value, list):
2617	            return [scrub_value(v, key, ctx) for v in value]
2618	        if isinstance(value, str):
2619	            if key in KEEP_STRING_KEYS:
2620	                return value
2621	            ctx["scrubbed"] += 1
2622	            return PLACEHOLDER
2623	        return value  # numbers, booleans, null

2733	    /**
2734	     * WP-06 — golden-fixture cross-validation (docs/PLAN.md §5).
2735	     *
2736	     * Every fixture listed in `fixtures/fixtures.json` is parsed and analyzed by
2737	     * the real engine and compared with the golden emitted by the independent
2738	     * Python reference sim (`tools/refsim/refsim.py`). Neither side is the
2739	     * oracle: a disagreement is settled by hand computation, never by copying
2740	     * one side's number into the other.
2741	     *
2742	     * Also enforced here, per fixture:
2743	     *  - the format-drift canary: the Claude Code versions a fixture carries
2744	     *    must sit inside `VALIDATED_VERSION_RANGE`, except the one fixture the
2745	     *    manifest marks `canaryExempt`, which must instead take the warn path;
2746	     *  - the content-poison property: the CONTENT_POISON marker every fixture
2747	     *    plants in conversation content never appears in any output;
2748	     *  - the reconciliation property: the scenario at the observed TTL
2749	     *    reproduces the actual cost;
2750	     *  - manifest completeness: every committed `.jsonl` under `fixtures/` is

2767	    const GOLDEN_SCHEMA = 'cache-ttl-analyzer/golden/v1'

2770	    /**
2771	     * Dollar figures are sums of per-token products; the two implementations
2772	     * sum in different orders, so allow floating-point noise and nothing more.
2773	     * (A single-token disagreement at the cheapest rate in the config —
2774	     * Haiku 3.5 cache reads, $0.08/MTok — is 8e-8, three orders above this.)
2775	     */

2777	    
2778	    interface ManifestEntry {
2779	      path: string
2780	      canaryExempt?: boolean
2781	      trap?: string
2782	      expect?: string
2783	    }
2784	    
2785	    interface Fixture extends ManifestEntry {
2786	      id: string
2787	      file: string
2788	    }
2789	    
2790	    type Json = null | boolean | number | string | Json[] | { [key: string]: Json }
2791	    
2792	    function loadFixtures(): Fixture[] {
2793	      const manifest = JSON.parse(readFileSync(MANIFEST_PATH, 'utf8')) as { fixtures: ManifestEntry[] }
2794	      const fixtures: Fixture[] = []
2795	      for (const entry of manifest.fixtures) {
2796	        const target = join(FIXTURES_ROOT, entry.path)
2797	        if (statSync(target).isDirectory()) {
2798	          for (const file of walk(target)) {
2799	            const rel = relative(FIXTURES_ROOT, file).replaceAll('\\', '/')
2800	            fixtures.push({ ...entry, id: rel.slice(0, -'.jsonl'.length), file })
2801	          }
2802	        } else {
2803	          fixtures.push({ ...entry, id: entry.path.slice(0, -'.jsonl'.length), file: target })
2804	        }
2805	      }
2806	      return fixtures
2807	    }
2808	    
2809	    function walk(dir: string): string[] {
2810	      const out: string[] = []
2811	      for (const name of readdirSync(dir).sort()) {
2812	        const path = join(dir, name)
2813	        if (statSync(path).isDirectory()) out.push(...walk(path))
2814	        else if (name.endsWith('.jsonl')) out.push(path)
2815	      }
2816	      return out
2817	    }
2818	    
2819	    function goldenPath(id: string): string {
2820	      return join(GOLDEN_DIR, `${id.replaceAll('/', '__')}.json`)
2821	    }
2822	    
2823	    async function runEngine(file: string) {
2824	      const parsed = await parseSession(
2825	        Readable.toWeb(createReadStream(file)) as ReadableStream<Uint8Array>,
2826	        { fileName: 'session.jsonl', fileSizeBytes: statSync(file).size, knownModels: knownModelIds(PRICING) },
2827	      )
2828	      if (parsed.verdict === 'not-a-session-log') {
2829	        return { parsed, result: undefined, reason: rejectionReason(parsed) }
2830	      }
2831	      return { parsed, result: analyzeSession(parsed, PRICING), reason: undefined }
2832	    }

3302	[tool use: Write]
3303	  file_path: ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer/scripts/capture-scenarios.sh
3304	  content:
3305	    #!/usr/bin/env bash
3306	    # WP-06 — enact the scenario sessions that become real captures (docs/PLAN.md
3307	    # WP-06, kind 2) by driving `claude -p` under a controlled prompt-cache TTL.
3308	    #
3309	    # Each scenario runs in its OWN throwaway working directory: Claude Code's
3310	    # cache is scoped to machine + directory, so two scenarios sharing a
3311	    # directory would read each other's cache and corrupt both captures. The
3312	    # directories hold a tiny read-only toy project so tool calls have something
3313	    # to look at; only read tools are allowed, so nothing outside is touched.
3314	    #
3315	    # Scenarios (all on the main-conversation bucket, `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL`):
3316	    #   tight-loop-5m   5m TTL, 8 back-to-back turns             → 5m should win
3317	    #   gap-heavy-1h    1h TTL, turns separated by 6m / 8m / 12m  → 1h should win (shortening path)
3318	    #   gap-heavy-5m    5m TTL, the same gaps                      → real expiries in the log; 1h should win (lengthening path)
3319	    #   model-switch    1h TTL, opus-5 → sonnet-5, then effort high → medium  → hard resets
3320	    #
3321	    # The two gap-heavy scenarios run concurrently (~28 min wall clock); the
3322	    # others take a couple of minutes each. Total ≈ 35 minutes.
3323	    #
3324	    # Usage:  scripts/capture-scenarios.sh [probe|all|<scenario>]
3325	    #   probe  — one 5m turn, then prints the cache_creation split so you can
3326	    #            confirm the env var is honored before spending a full run.
3327	    # Output: session ids and log paths are written to $OUT/summary.txt; run
3328	    # scripts/scrub-capture.py on each log afterwards.
3329	    
3330	    set -euo pipefail
3331	    
3332	    OUT="${CAPTURE_OUT:-${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/cache-ttl-captures}"
3333	    PROJECTS="$HOME/.claude/projects"
3334	    MODEL_A="claude-opus-5"
3335	    MODEL_B="claude-sonnet-5"
3336	    TOOLS="Read,Glob,Grep"
3337	    mkdir -p "$OUT"
3338	    SUMMARY="$OUT/summary.txt"
3339	    
3340	    log() { printf '%s %s\n' "$(date -u +%H:%M:%S)" "$*" | tee -a "$OUT/run.log" >&2; }
3341	    
3342	    # A tiny toy project, identical for every scenario.
3343	    make_project() {
3344	      local dir="$1"
3345	      mkdir -p "$dir/src" "$dir/tests"
3346	      cat >"$dir/README.md" <<'EOF'
3347	    # tinycache
3348	    
3349	    A toy in-memory LRU cache with TTL support. Used as a fixture project for
3350	    cache-ttl-analyzer scenario captures; nothing here is real.
3351	    EOF
3352	      cat >"$dir/src/lru.py" <<'EOF'
3353	    """A small LRU cache with per-entry TTL."""
3354	    import time
3355	    from collections import OrderedDict
3356	    
3357	    
3358	    class LRU:
3359	        def __init__(self, capacity=128, ttl_seconds=300):
3360	            self.capacity = capacity
3361	            self.ttl = ttl_seconds
3362	            self._d = OrderedDict()
3363	    
3364	        def get(self, key):
3365	            item = self._d.get(key)
3366	            if item is None:
3367	                return None
3368	            value, expires = item
3369	            if expires < time.monotonic():
3370	                del self._d[key]  # TODO: count expirations
3371	                return None
3372	            self._d.move_to_end(key)
3373	            return value
3374	    
3375	        def put(self, key, value):
3376	            self._d[key] = (value, time.monotonic() + self.ttl)
3377	            self._d.move_to_end(key)
3378	            while len(self._d) > self.capacity:
3379	                self._d.popitem(last=False)
3380	    
3381	        def stats(self):
3382	            # TODO: hits / misses / expirations
3383	            return {"size": len(self._d)}
3384	    EOF
3385	      cat >"$dir/src/cli.py" <<'EOF'
3386	    """CLI: tinycache put KEY VALUE | get KEY"""
3387	    import sys
3388	    from lru import LRU
3389	    
3390	    cache = LRU()
3391

3394	        if not argv:

3402	            v = cache.get(rest[0])

3439	[tool use: Write]
3440	  file_path: ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer/fixtures/README.md
3441	  content:
3442	    # Fixtures and golden data (WP-06)
3443	    
3444	    Test inputs for the analysis engine, and the expected outputs the engine must
3445	    reproduce. The build plan's testing strategy is in
3446	    [docs/PLAN.md §5](../docs/PLAN.md#5-testing-strategy); this directory is how it
3447	    is wired.
3448	    
3449	    ```
3450	    fixtures/
3451	      fixtures.json        the manifest: every fixture, what trap it exercises, what to expect
3452	      synthetic/*.jsonl    crafted sessions, one trap each          (scripts/build-synthetic-fixtures.ts)
3453	      adversarial/*.jsonl  hostile or non-session inputs            (same script)
3454	      captured/<name>/     real Claude Code sessions, content-scrubbed (scripts/scrub-capture.py)
3455	      golden/*.json        expected output per fixture              (tools/refsim/refsim.py emit)
3456	      generated/           the 100MB parser stress fixture — built at test time, git-ignored
3457	    ```
3458	    
3459	    ## How the pieces fit
3460	    
3461	    1. **Fixtures** are session logs. Synthetic ones are generated by a script so
3462	       they are reviewable as code; captured ones are real sessions with every
3463	       conversation payload replaced by a placeholder (see *Scrubbing* below).
3464	    2. **Goldens** are produced by the Python reference simulator in
3465	       [`tools/refsim/`](../tools/refsim/) — an independently written second
3466	       implementation of the parser, cost engine and counterfactual model. One
3467	       golden per fixture, named `<fixture id with / → __>.json`.
3468	    3. **The harness** `src/engine/golden.test.ts` runs the TypeScript engine over
3469	       every fixture and compares with its golden, field by field, with a
3470	       floating-point tolerance of 1e-10 on dollar figures (the two
3471	       implementations sum in different orders; any real disagreement is orders
3472	       of magnitude larger). It also enforces, per fixture:
3473	       - the **format-drift canary**: the Claude Code `version` a fixture carries
3474	         must be inside `VALIDATED_VERSION_RANGE` (`src/engine/contract.ts`),
3475	         except the one fixture marked `canaryExempt`, which must take the
3476	         warn-don't-fail path instead;
3477	       - the **content-poison property**: the marker `POISON` planted in every
3478	         conversation payload never appears in the parser output, the analysis, or
3479	         the golden;
3480	       - the **reconciliation property**: replaying at the observed TTL reproduces
3481	         the actual cost (exact for every bucket except a 5m-dominant bucket that
3482	         also carries 1h writes — a mid-session config flip, where the "5m"
3483	         scenario deliberately reprices the flipped writes);
3484	       - **manifest completeness**: every `.jsonl` under `fixtures/` is listed.
3485	    4. **CI** runs the reference sim's own hand-computed tests and
3486	       `refsim.py check`, which re-derives every golden and fails on any byte of
3487	       difference — so a golden can never drift from the sim that owns it.
3488	    
3489	    Neither implementation is the oracle. When they disagree, the number is worked
3490	    out by hand and the side that is wrong gets fixed; goldens are never edited to
3491	    make a test pass.
3492	    
3493	    ## Commands
3494	    
3495	    ```sh
3496	    npm run fixtures:build   # regenerate synthetic/ and adversarial/ from the script
3497	    npm run golden:emit      # (re)write every golden from the reference sim
3498	    npm run golden:check     # verify the committed goldens are current (CI)
3499	    npm run test:refsim      # the reference sim's own unit tests
3500	    npm test                 # includes the golden harness
3501	    ```
3502	    
3503	    Adding a fixture: drop the `.jsonl` in place (or add a case to
3504	    `scripts/build-synthetic-fixtures.ts`), list it in `fixtures.json` with a
3505	    `trap` and an `expect`, run `npm run golden:emit`, then check that the golden
3506	    says what `expect` claims before committing.
3507	    
3508	    ## Synthetic fixtures

3511	    it exercises and the behavior the golden should show. Rows mirror real
3512	    v2.1.251 logs: `user → attachment → assistant` chains, one assistant row per
3513	    content block with the full usage on each, realistic `usage` subfields.
3514	    Timestamps start at `2026-08-30T12:00:00Z`; prices are Opus 5 unless the trap
3515	    is about models.
3516	    
3517	    The adversarial set covers the validation rules in
3518	    [docs/PLAN.md §2](../docs/PLA
3519	    ... [2857 characters truncated]

3524	[tool use: Write]
3525	  file_path: ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer/tools/refsim/README.md
3526	  content:
3527	    # Reference simulator
3528	    
3529	    `refsim.py` is the **independently written second implementation** of the
3530	    analysis engine (docs/PLAN.md decision D7): a plain-Python parser, cost engine
3531	    and counterfactual simulator that follows the same spec as `src/engine/` —
3532	    the §2 correctness-rules table, the frozen contract, and the WP-05
3533	    implementation notes — but shares no code with it. Its only shared input is
3534	    `src/config/pricing.json`.
3535	    
3536	    It exists to emit the golden files under `fixtures/golden/` that the
3537	    TypeScript engine must reproduce (`src/engine/golden.test.ts`). Neither
3538	    implementation is trusted over the other: `test_refsim.py` holds hand-computed
3539	    expectations (arithmetic written next to each number), and those are the
3540	    tiebreaker when the two disagree.
3541	    
3542	    It replaces the throwaway `prototype-sim.py` from the feasibility research,
3543	    which implemented almost none of the rules and was never a valid oracle.
3544	    
3545	    ```sh
3546	    python3 tools/refsim/refsim.py analyze path/to/session.jsonl   # golden JSON to stdout
3547	    npm run golden:emit                                            # write every golden
3548	    npm run golden:check                                           # fail if any golden is stale (CI)
3549	    npm run test:refsim                                            # hand-computed unit tests
3550	    ```
3551	    
3552	    No dependencies beyond the standard library; Python ≥ 3.10.
3553	    
3554	    ## What it implements
3555	    
3556	    - **Parsing** (`SessionParser`): line splitting with the same semantics as the
3557	      engine's byte splitter (every `\n` ends a line, trailing `\r` and a leading
3558	      BOM stripped, over-cap lines counted as malformed and capped); JSON parsing
3559	      that rejects `NaN`/`Infinity` literals like `JSON.parse`; dedup on
3560	      `message.id` (first row defines the request, later rows extend the
3561	      completion timestamp); `<synthetic>` exclusion; numeric hygiene (absent/null
3562	      = 0, otherwise a non-negative safe integer); request start via the
3563	      `parentUuid` walk to the nearest `user` row; thread keys from `agentId`,
3564	      else legacy sidechain recovery from `parentUuid` chain roots; metadata
3565	      sanitization (control characters stripped, clamp in UTF-16 units, dangling
3566	      surrogate dropped); the three validation verdicts and every warning kind.
3567	    - **Pricing**: base × tier × speed multipliers, cache read/write multipliers,
3568	      unattributed writes at 5m, unknown models unpriced.
3569	    - **Simulation**: per-bucket, per-thread replay under each TTL with gaps
3570	      between request starts; hard resets on model/effort/version; the
3571	      mixed-TTL policy (server-tool 5m share held at 5m in a 1h-dominant bucket;
3572	      everything user-controlled in a 5m-dominant one); shortening and
3573	      lengthening edits; the insight-event timeline; session shape; verdicts and
3574	      suppression.
3575	    
3576	    ## Keeping it independent
3577	    
3578	    When a golden disagreement shows up, do not fix it by reading the other
3579	    implementation's code path and copying it. Work the case out on paper (add it
3580	    to `test_refsim.py` or `src/engine/simulator.test.ts` as a hand-computed
3581	    test), then fix whichever side is wrong.
3582

4657	    rows = s[i:j] + """
4658	    | `captured/scenarios/tight-loop-5m/` | Claude-enacted: eight back-to-back prompts on a toy project with `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL=5m` (13 requests, largest gap 18s). | The 5m-configured capture the plan asked for. 5m wins ($0.406 vs $0.473); no expiries in either scenario. |
4659	    | `captured/scenarios/gap-heavy-1h/` | Four prompts at 1h separated by 6m / 8m / 12m of idle time (9 requests). | The shortening direction on real data: the 5m scenario adds three expiries; 1h wins ($0.32 vs $0.70). |
4660	    | `captured/scenarios/gap-heavy-5m/` | The same prompts and gaps with the TTL set to 5m (8 requests). | The lapse-heavy path on real data — and a **partial-invalidation** finding, below. 5m wins narrowly ($0.562 vs $0.579). |
4661	    | `captured/scenarios/model-switch/` | Two turns on Opus 5, `/model` to Sonnet 5, then effort high → medium, all at 1h (10 requests). | Two hard resets (`model-change`, `effort-change`) that no elapsed time explains; per-request pricing across two models. |
4662	    
4663	    The scenarios were recorded by `scripts/capture-scenarios.sh`, which drives
4664	    `claude -p` in one throwaway directory per scenario (the cache is scoped to
4665	    machine + directory, so scenarios must not share one) with read-only tools,
4666	    then scrubbed like any other capture. They double as the app's bundled
4667	    samples: `scripts/sync-samples.ts` copies them to `public/samples/` and
4668	    derives `index.json` (title, description, expected verdict) from each
4669	    capture's `capture.json` and golden, and the harness asserts the copies are
4670	    byte-identical.
4671	    
4672	    ### Finding: partial invalidation, live
4673	    
4674	    `gap-heavy-5m` shows what feasibility §7 predicted but no corpus session had
4675	    exhibited. After the first long gap (364s) the log has `read 0` and a full
4676	    re-write (25,469 tokens) — the all-or-nothing lapse. After the next two gaps
4677	    (487s, 730s) it has `read 11,808` *and* a ~14k re-write: a stable prefix
4678	    breakpoint stayed warm while the conversation tail lapsed. The WP-05
4679	    lengthening rule only restores a re-write when the request read nothing, so
4680	    the 1h counterfactual recovers the full lapse but not the two partial ones —
4681	    and 1h loses a session that, by the 1h run's numbers, it should win. That is
4682	    a modeling limit to revisit in the simulator (restore `min(write, warm −
4683	    read)` on partial lapses, or similar); until then the tool under-sells 1h on
4684	    5m-configured sessions with partial lapses, which is the opposite direction
4685	    from the §7 conservatism disclosed in the UI. The golden pins today's
4686	    behavior so the change, when made, is visible."""
4687	    s = s[:i] + rows + s[j:]
4688	    p.write_text(s)
