5336	------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5337	Base directory for this skill: ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer/.claude/skills/publish-transcript
5338	
5339	# Publish a session transcript
5340	
5341	Turns one Claude Code session for this repo into one committed folder holding
5342	both published forms:
5343	
5344	```
5345	transcripts/<NNN-slug>/
5346	  session.jsonl    the redacted session log
5347	  index.html       rendered via simonw/claude-code-transcripts
5348	  page-001.html
5349	```
5350	
5351	**The HTML is rendered from the redacted JSONL, never from the original.**
5352	Redact once at the source and both outputs stay consistent — never redact the
5353	rendered HTML separately.
5354	
5355	`to_text.py` renders a session as plain text for *your* review in step 2. That
5356	rendering is a working file: it is never committed. Only `session.jsonl` and the
5357	rendered HTML go into the repo.
5358	
5359	## Prerequisites
5360	
5361	`claude-code-transcripts` must be on PATH. If `which claude-code-transcripts`
5362	comes up empty, run it via `uvx claude-code-transcripts` instead, or install with
5363	`uv tool install claude-code-transcripts`.
5364	
5365	Scripts live in `.claude/skills/publish-transcript/scripts/`. Below, `$S` means
5366	that directory and `$W` a scratch working directory (use the session scratchpad,
5367	not the repo — intermediate files must never be committed).
5368	
5369	## Step 1 — Pick the session
5370	
5371	```bash
5372	python3 $S/list_sessions.py --limit 20
5373	```
5374	
5375	Show the list and ask the user which session to publish, unless they already
5376	named one (by session id, by "the current session", or by describing its topic
5377	clearly enough to match exactly one row).
5378	
5379	**Publishing the current session:** the JSONL is still being written, so it will
5380	not contain this final turn. That is expected and fine — say so, and offer to
5381	re-run later if they want the tail included. Note that the skill is loaded at
5382	session start, so a session that *created or edited* the skill cannot invoke it
5383	by name; follow these steps directly instead.
5384	
5385	## Step 2 — Stage and render for review
5386	
5387	```bash
5388	mkdir -p $W
5389	cp ~/.claude/projects/<project-slug>/<session-id>.jsonl $W/original.jsonl
5390	python3 $S/to_text.py $W/original.jsonl -o $W/review.txt --max-tool-output 4000
5391	```
5392	
5393	`list_sessions.py` prints the absolute source path — copy it from there rather
5394	than reconstructing it.
5395	
5396	## Step 3 — Find sensitive material
5397	
5398	Two passes, both required. The regex pass catches well-shaped secrets; the
5399	reading pass catches everything shaped like ordinary prose.
5400	
5401	**Pass A — regex triage:**
5402	
5403	```bash
5404	python3 $S/scan_secrets.py $W/original.jsonl
5405	```
5406	
5407	**Pass B — read the transcript.** Read `$W/review.txt` in full. For a long
5408	session, read it in chunks — do not skim, and do not delegate this to a subagent
5409	that only reports back a summary. You are looking for things a regex cannot
5410	match:
5411	
5412	- Credentials, tokens, API keys, passwords, connection strings — including ones
5413	  pasted mid-sentence or shown in command output
5414	- Real names, emails, phone numbers, addresses of anyone (the user included)
5415	- Customer, client, or employer names the user may not want public
5416	- Internal hostnames, private URLs, ticket IDs, Slack/DM links, S3 buckets
5417	- **Names of the user's other projects.** A session that ran `ls ~/.claude/projects`,
5418	  `git remote -v` elsewhere, or any directory listing above the repo will name
5419	  unrelated work. A regex cannot catch this — it is the single most likely real
5420	  leak in an otherwise clean session.
5421	- Unreleased or confidential product details discussed in passing
5422	- `.env` file contents, private keys, or `~/.aws/credentials` echoed by a tool
5423	- Anything in a file the repo's `.gitignore` excludes (this repo ignores
5424	  `.env`, `.env.*`) — if its contents appear in the transcript, redact them
5425	
5426	Judgment calls to raise with the user rather than decide alone:
5427	
5428	- **The user's own name and email.** They are already in the git history of this
5429	  repo, so redacting them is usually pointless — but ask, don't assume.
5430	- **Home directory paths** (`/Users/<name>/...`). Low risk, but they leak a
5431	  username and local layout. Offer to rewrite them to `~/`.
5432	- **Third parties' names.** Default to redacting these; a person who was
5433	  mentioned in a session did not consent to being published.
5434	
5435	## Step 4 — Propose redactions
5436	
5437	Write `$W/redactions.json` — a list of rules, each with a `reason`:
5438	
5439	```json
5440	[
5441	  {"find": "sk-ant-api03-REAL-KEY", "replace": "[REDACTED_API_KEY]", "reason": "Anthropic API key in tool output"},
5442	  {"find": "coworker@example.com", "replace": "[REDACTED_EMAIL]", "reason": "third party PII"},
5443	  {"find": "~", "replace": "~", "reason": "local home path"}
5444	]
5445	```
5446	
5447	Rules are literal by default; add `"regex": true` for a pattern. Use
5448	`[REDACTED_<KIND>]` placeholders so a reader can tell *what* was removed.
5449	
5450	**Redact the shortest distinctive token, not the full prefixed value.** A
5451	transcript contains *truncated* copies of its own content — tool output gets cut
5452	off, scanner context windows clip mid-value, and prose refers to values with an
5453	ellipsis. A rule for `sk-ant-api03-<key>` will sail past `...-api03-<key>` and
5454	`sk-ant-api03-<pre>...`, leaving fragments behind. Add a rule for the bare core
5455	(`<key>`) as well as the full value, and for a multi-part name like
5456	`acme-widget-service` add one for `acme` too.
5457	
5458	**Watch for self-reference.** Once a session discusses its own redaction, the
5459	sensitive strings appear in the discussion — in your grep commands, your
5460	findings summary, and your questions to the user. Re-copying the source JSONL
5461	after that point pulls those in, and each re-copy adds more. Copy the source
5462	once, redact and verify against that snapshot, and stop; do not chase the tail.
5463	
5464	Present the proposed list to the user — grouped by category, with the reason and
5465	match count for each — and get approval before applying. **If you find a live
5466	credential, tell the user plainly that it should be rotated**, since it existed
5467	in plaintext on disk regardless of what gets published.
5468	
5469	If both passes come up clean, say so explicitly and confirm they want to publish
5470	unredacted rather than silently proceeding.
5471	
5472	## Step 5 — Apply and verify
5473	
5474	```bash
5475	python3 $S/apply_redactions.py $W/original.jsonl $W/redactions.json \
5476	    -o $W/redacted.jsonl --verify
5477	python3 $S/scan_secrets.py $W/redacted.jsonl
5478	```
5479	
5480	`--verify` fails if any literal target survived. Investigate a `NO MATCHES`
5481	warning before continuing — it usually means a typo'd rule, which means
5482	something you meant to redact is still in the file.
5483	
5484	The second scan is the real check: re-read its output and confirm every
5485	remaining hit is something you and the user consciously decided to keep.
5486	
5487	## Step 6 — Render the two artifacts
5488	
5489	Name the session `NNN-short-topic`: a zero-padded counter one higher than the
5490	highest folder already in `transcripts/`, plus a kebab-case topic — e.g.
5491	`002-cost-model`.
5492	
5493	`--json` copies the JSONL into the output directory alongside the HTML, naming
5494	it after the input file. Rename the redacted file to `session.jsonl` first so it
5495	lands with the right name, and the whole folder is produced in one command:
5496	
5497	```bash
5498	SLUG=<NNN-short-topic>
5499	mv $W/redacted.jsonl $W/session.jsonl
5500	claude-code-transcripts json $W/session.jsonl -o transcripts/$SLUG --json
5501	```
5502	
5503	Do not pass `--gist` — that uploads to a public Gist, which is a separate
5504	publishing decision the user has to make explicitly.
5505	
5506	Then confirm the redaction survived rendering:
5507	
5508	```bash
5509	python3 $S/scan_secrets.py transcripts/$SLUG/*
5510	```
5511	
5512	## Step 7 — Update the session map
5513	
5514	Add a row to the table in `transcripts/README.md`:
5515	
5516	| Date | Session | Covers | Share link | Notes |
5517	|---|---|---|---|---|
5518	| 2026-08-30 | [001-transcript-skill](001-transcript-skill/) | Built the transcript publishing skill | [open](https://cebert.github.io/cache-ttl-analyzer/001-transcript-skill/) | 3 emails redacted |
5519	
5520	Keep rows in date order. Put a short, honest note about what was redacted in the
5521	Notes column — that is the reader's signal that gaps are deliberate.
5522	
5523	The share link is the GitHub Pages copy —
5524	`https://cebert.github.io/cache-ttl-analyzer/<slug>/`, live once the commit
5525	reaches `main`. This table is also the source for that site's landing page, so
5526	a missing or misspelled row fails the `Transcripts site` workflow rather than
5527	publishing a broken index. Check the table still matches the folders with:
5528	
5529	```bash
5530	npm run build:transcripts
5531	```
5532	
5533	**Ask the user what the session covers — don't just write it yourself.** Propose
5534	a one-line summary drawn from reading the transcript, and ask them to confirm or
5535	replace it. They know why the session mattered; the transcript only shows what
5536	happened in it.
5537	
5538	Aim the summary at *why the work was done*, not the mechanics of what was
5539	typed. Where a session advanced a stated goal of the project, say so — that
5540	connection is the reason the transcript is worth keeping, and it is exactly the
5541	part a reader cannot reconstruct from the log.
5542	
5543	- Weak: "Created four Python scripts and a SKILL.md"
5544	- Better: "Built the transcript publishing skill, so build sessions can be
5545	  published without leaking secrets or PII — a stated goal of the project"
5546	
5547	Use the same confirmed summary for the commit subject in step 8, so the map row
5548	and the git history agree.
5549	
5550	## Step 8 — Commit
5551	
5552	This repo uses [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/).
5553	Transcript publishing is a `docs` change:
5554	
5555	```bash
5556	git add transcripts/
5557	git commit -m "docs(transcripts): add session log for <topic>"
5558	```
5559	
5560	Guidance:
5561	
5562	- Subject in imperative mood, no trailing period, under ~72 characters.
5563	- If anything was redacted, note it in the body — e.g.
5564	  `Redacted 2 API keys and 3 third-party email addresses before publishing.`
5565	- Commit only under `transcripts/`; leave unrelated working-tree changes alone.
5566	- Do not push unless the user asks. Publishing to a remote is their call, and it
5567	  is the point of no return for anything that slipped through redaction.
5568

4148	  file_path: ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer-wp06/scripts/sync-samples.ts
4149	  content:
4150	    /**
4151	     * WP-06 — publish the scenario captures as the app's bundled samples.
4152	     *
4153	     * Copies every `fixtures/captured/scenarios/<name>/session.jsonl` to
4154	     * `public/samples/<name>.jsonl` and writes `public/samples/index.json`, the
4155	     * list the sample loader (WP-07) reads. Titles and descriptions come from
4156	     * each capture's `capture.json`; the expected verdict comes from the
4157	     * capture's golden, so the index can never disagree with the engine.
4158	     *
4159	     * Run: `node scripts/sync-samples.ts`. `src/engine/golden.test.ts` asserts
4160	     * the copies are byte-identical to their fixtures and the index is current.
4161	     */
4162	    
4163	    import { copyFileSync, existsSync, mkdirSync, readdirSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs'
4164	    import { join } from 'node:path'
4165	    
4166	    export const SCENARIOS_DIR = join('fixtures', 'captured', 'scenarios')
4167	    export const SAMPLES_DIR = join('public', 'samples')
4168	    export const SAMPLES_INDEX = join(SAMPLES_DIR, 'index.json')
4169	    
4170	    export interface SampleEntry {
4171	      /** File name under public/samples/. */
4172	      file: string
4173	      title: string
4174	      description: string
4175	      /** The fixture this sample is a copy of, relative to the repo root. */
4176	      source: string
4177	      claudeCodeVersions: string[]
4178	      /** Main-bucket verdict from the golden, so the UI can promise what the sample shows. */
4179	      expectedRecommendation: string
4180	      observedTtl: string | null
4181	      requestCount: number
4182	    }
4183	    
4184	    export interface SamplesIndex {
4185	      $comment: string
4186	      samples: SampleEntry[]
4187	    }
4188	    
4189	    interface CaptureJson {
4190	      title: string
4191	      description: string
4192	      source: { claudeCodeVersions: string[] }
4193	    }
4194	    
4195	    interface Golden {
4196	      outcome: string
4197	      buckets?: { bucket: string; recommendation: string; observedTtl: string | null; requestCount: number }[]
4198	    }
4199	    
4200	    export function buildSamplesIndex(): SamplesIndex {
4201	      const samples: SampleEntry[] = []
4202	      if (!existsSync(SCENARIOS_DIR)) return { $comment: comment(), samples }
4203	      for (const name of readdirSync(SCENARIOS_DIR).sort()) {
4204	        const dir = join(SCENARIOS_DIR, name)
4205	        const session = join(dir, 'session.jsonl')
4206	        if (!existsSync(session)) continue
4207	        const capture = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dir, 'capture.json'), 'utf8')) as CaptureJson
4208	        const goldenPath = join('fixtures', 'golden', `captured__scenarios__${name}__session.json`)
4209	        const golden = JSON.parse(readFileSync(goldenPath, 'utf8')) as Golden
4210	        const main = golden.buckets?.find((b) => b.bucket === 'main')
4211	        if (golden.outcome !== 'analysis' || !main) {
4212	          throw new Error(`${name}: a bundled sample must analyze cleanly (golden says ${golden.outcome})`)
4213	        }
4214	        samples.push({
4215	          file: `${name}.jsonl`,
4216	          title: capture.title,
4217	          description: capture.description,
4218	          source: session.replaceAll('\\', '/'),
4219	          claudeCodeVersions: capture.source.claudeCodeVersions,
4220	          expectedRecommendation: main.recommendation,
4221	          observedTtl: main.observedTtl,
4222	          requestCount: main.requestCount,
4223	        })
4224	      }
4225	      return { $comment: comment(), samples }
4226	    }
4227	    
4228	    function comment(): string {
4229	      return 'Generated by scripts/sync-samples.ts from fixtures/captured/scenarios/ — do not edit by hand.'
4230	    }
4231	    
4232	    export function renderIndex(index: SamplesIndex): string {
4233	      return JSON.stringify(index, null, 2) + '\n'
4234	    }
4235	    
4236	    function main(): void {
4237	      mkdirSync(SAMPLES_DIR, { recursive: true })
4238	      const index = buildSamplesIndex()
4239	      for (const sample of index.samples) {
4240	        copyFileSync(sample.source, join(SAMPLES_DIR, sample.file))
4241	        console.log(`copied ${sample.source} -> ${join(SAMPLES_DIR, sample.file)}`)
4242	      }
4243	      writeFileSync(SAMPLES_INDEX, renderIndex(index))
4244	      console.log(`wrote ${SAMPLES_INDEX} (${index.samples.length} samples)`)
4245	    }
4246	    
4247	    // Only run when executed directly, so the test can import the helpers.
4248	    if (process.argv[1]?.endsWith('sync-samples.ts')) main()

Assistant2026-08-31T00:54:52.532Z

Yes — PR #17 is ready to merge. https://github.com/cebert/cache-ttl-analyzer/pull/17

All checks are green on the final commit (c360ba4): lint/typecheck/test/build (484 tests, including WP-07's), the new Python step (29 reference-sim tests + golden:check over 60 goldens), the transcripts-site build, and the preview deploy. Working tree is clean and main (PR #18) is merged in.

What the PR delivers (WP-06): - tools/refsim/ — the prototype rewritten to spec parity as an independent Python reference sim, with hand-computed tests; it emits the goldens, it isn't the oracle. - 22 synthetic + 6 adversarial fixtures (generated, committed), a scrubbed 27-subagent capture, and four Claude-enacted scenario captures; 60 goldens; a manifest with the trap and expectation per fixture. - src/engine/golden.test.ts: engine-vs-golden diff plus the format-drift canary, content-poison, reconciliation, manifest-completeness, and sample-drift checks. - public/samples/ filled via WP-07's SAMPLES (three samples, one per lesson the landing page teaches), with card numbers asserted against the fixtures. - PLAN.md: WP-05/WP-06 complete, D20/D21 (scrub-to-metadata, inference_geo assumption), WP-04 gap closed by assumption. - Transcript 011-wp-06-fixtures published (email, name, one unrelated project name redacted; paths/username rewritten).

Two things worth your eyes before or after merging: 1. Partial invalidation, seen live in gap-heavy-5m: after 8m/12m gaps a ~11.8k stable prefix stayed warm while the tail lapsed. The WP-05 lengthening rule needs reads == 0, so 1h loses a session it should win ($0.562 vs $0.579). I left the simulator unchanged and documented it as a follow-up (fixtures/README.md, PLAN.md) — it's a modeling decision for you, and the golden pins today's behavior so the fix will be visible. That capture ships as a fixture only, not a sample. 2. The reconciliation property is exact everywhere except a 5m-dominant bucket with flipped 1h writes, by design (hand-checked, $0.004125).

Housekeeping: I worked from a worktree at ../cache-ttl-analyzer-wp06 after another session switched branches in the main checkout; the main checkout is back on ci/transcripts-github-pages at its pushed commit, untouched.

User2026-08-31T00:59:11.030Z

this analysis is great, this tool looks useful actually

Assistant2026-08-31T00:59:16.423Z

Thanks — and I think the captures are the strongest argument for it. Two sessions with the same four prompts and the same three idle gaps came out at $0.32 (1h) versus $0.56 (5m) — a 75% difference on nothing but a config setting nobody sees. The tool's whole job is making that visible from a log file people already have on disk, and the real data says the effect is not subtle.

The other thing the captures bought you is credibility of the model itself: the partial-invalidation case is exactly the kind of thing a synthetic fixture would never have produced, and now it's pinned in the harness waiting for the simulator fix. When you get to that follow-up, the natural rule is "on a >5m gap with reads > 0 and a large write, restore min(write, warm − read) as reads" — and gap-heavy-5m's golden will flip to 1h wins the moment it's right.

PR #17 is ready whenever you want to merge.
